**[00:03:12]** And we looked at many biblical examples and precedents that God has set.

**[00:03:27]** Meaning if you can determine a precedent that God has set, you can understand how he

**[00:03:38]** Like when Jesus taught, for example, for example, the word pornography is not in the Bible,

**[00:04:36]** And one thing is very clear.

**[00:04:38]** Actually several things are clear.

**[00:04:40]** God always gives an appropriate punishment.

**[00:04:43]** Pain for pain with of course the maximum punishment being death.

**[00:04:48]** It's always been eye for eye, tooth for tooth.

**[00:04:52]** Often it was poetic justice, which was very much related to what the person did.

**[00:04:55]** Like.

**[00:04:57]** David cheated with Bathsheba and God as punishment made it to where his wives would be taken

**[00:05:06]** out in public.

**[00:05:08]** David did this thing in secret.

**[00:05:11]** He was misrepresenting God by doing that.

**[00:05:13]** So his punishment is equal pain for equal pain in the sense, in this sense, it's also

**[00:05:17]** poetic justice because God is going to have his wives out in the open with his enemies.

**[00:05:24]** And so.

**[00:05:25]** Yeah.

**[00:05:26]** So it's one of those types of situations.
